u_{n,m}=\left[\frac{mn}{(m-1)\log{n}}\right]^{1/{\alpha}}
and
\beta_{n}=\left(\frac{\log{n}}{n}\right)^{1/{\alpha}}
, we get that

{y^{\prime}}_{n}^{1}\sim\frac{\alpha\theta u_{n,m}^{2}a_{m}^{2-\alpha}\beta_{n}^{2-\alpha}}{(2-\alpha)nm^{2}}(\rho(\beta_{n}/\varepsilon^{\prime})-\rho({a^{\prime}}_{m}))\sim\frac{\alpha^{2}\theta^{2}u_{n,m}^{2}a_{m}^{2-\alpha}\beta_{n}^{2-\alpha}}{(2-\alpha)nm^{2}}\log{(\varepsilon^{\prime}/\beta_{n})}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\frac{\alpha\theta^{2}}{m(m-1)(2-\alpha)}

and that
{y^{\prime}}_{n}^{2}\leq[(1-{\beta_{n}}^{2})^{\frac{2-\alpha}{2}}-1]{y^{\prime}}_{n}^{1}
which converges to
0
. Therefore, clearly,
nK_{n,m}^{1}(h_{3})\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}K^{1}(h_{3})=\frac{\alpha\theta^{2}}{m(m-1)(2-\alpha)}
. Thanks to this proof, in particular we have proved that

\displaystyle\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})^{2}}{nm^{2}\lambda_{n,m}^{2}}\int_{1\geq|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\beta_{n}}u_{n,m}^{2}x^{2}y^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|y|\leq\frac{\sqrt{x^{-2}-1}}{u_{n,m}}\}}F(dx)F(dy)\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\frac{\alpha\theta^{2}}{m(m-1)(2-\alpha)}.

(4.16)

In conclusion, the obtained limit pair has independent marginals since it has no Gaussian part and its Lévy measure
K^{1}
is supported on the union of the coordinate axes (see e.g.
[
15
, Ex.12.8 ]
).

\bullet

Third, for fixed
k,k^{\prime},j,j^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,m\}
,
(j,k)\neq(j^{\prime},k^{\prime})
and
1\leq j\leq k-1
,
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq k^{\prime}-1
we consider the convergence of
\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\zeta_{i,j,k}^{n}(1),\zeta_{i,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}^{n}(1))
whose variables
(\zeta_{i,j,k}^{n}(1),\zeta_{i,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}^{n}(1))
,
{1\leq i\leq n}
are i.i.d. When
k
,
k^{\prime}
,
j
and
j^{\prime}
are different, we have straightforward the independence between the marginals of the limit pair. Otherwise, by symmetry of the roles played by
j
and
k
and the roles played by
j^{\prime}
and
k^{\prime}
, it is enough to consider the particular case where
k=k^{\prime}
and
j\neq j^{\prime}
. Note that the law of
(\zeta_{i,j,k}^{n}(1),\zeta_{i,j^{\prime},k}^{n}(1))
does not depend on parameters
k
and
j^{\prime}
, we denote its law by
K^{2}_{n,m}
. We will prove that
nK_{n,m}^{2}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}K^{2}
, where

K^{2}(dx,dy)=\frac{\theta^{2}\alpha}{2m(m-1)}\delta_{0}(dy)\frac{1}{|x|^{1+\alpha}}dx+\frac{\theta^{2}\alpha}{2m(m-1)}\delta_{0}(dx)\frac{1}{|y|^{1+\alpha}}dy

and
\delta_{0}
is Dirac measure sitting at point
0
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle nK^{2}_{n,m}(h)=n\mathbb{E}((\zeta_{i,j,k}^{n}(1),\zeta_{i,j^{\prime},k}^{n}(1))|\mathcal{F}_{t_{i}^{1}})

\displaystyle=n\mathbb{E}(h(0,0);K(t_{i}^{k})=0|\mathcal{F}_{t_{i}^{1}})+n\mathbb{E}(h(0,0);K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1,K(t^{j}_{i})=0,K(t^{j^{\prime}}_{i})=0|\mathcal{F}_{t_{i}^{1}})

\displaystyle+n\mathbb{E}(h(0,u_{n,m}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t^{j^{\prime}}_{i})});K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1,K(t^{j}_{i})=0,K(t^{j^{\prime}}_{i})\geq 1|\mathcal{F}_{t_{i}^{1}})

\displaystyle+n\mathbb{E}(h(u_{n,m}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t^{j}_{i})},0);K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1,K(t^{j}_{i})\geq 1,K(t^{j^{\prime}}_{i})=0|\mathcal{F}_{t_{i}^{1}})

\displaystyle+n\mathbb{E}(h(u_{n,m}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t^{j}_{i})},u_{n,m}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t^{j^{\prime}}_{i})});K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1,K(t^{j}_{i})\geq 1,K(t^{j^{\prime}}_{i})\geq 1|\mathcal{F}_{t_{i}^{1}})

\displaystyle=ne^{-\lambda_{n,m}}(1+e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}))h(0,0)

\displaystyle+\frac{e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})^{2}}{nm^{2}\lambda_{n,m}^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\beta_{n}}h(0,u_{n,m}xy)F(dx)F(dy)

\displaystyle+\frac{e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})^{2}}{nm^{2}\lambda_{n,m}^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\beta_{n}}h(u_{n,m}xy,0)F(dx)F(dy)

\displaystyle+\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})^{3}}{n^{2}m^{3}\lambda_{n,m}^{3}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|z|>\beta_{n}}h(u_{n,m}xy,u_{n,m}xz)F(dx)F(dy)F(dz).

Now, we verify the three elements in Lemma
C.7
with suitable choices of function
h
.

Concerning assertion (i)
:

We choose
h=h_{u,v}
where
h_{u,v}(x,y)=\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\geq u,|y|\geq v\}}
for all
u,v\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that
(u,v)\neq(0,0)
.

a)

For
u>0
and
v=0
,

nK_{n,m}^{2}(h_{u,0})=\frac{e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})^{2}}{nm^{2}\lambda_{n,m}^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\beta_{n}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|u_{n,m}xy|\geq u\}}F(dx)F(dy)\\
+\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})^{3}}{nm^{2}\lambda_{n,m}^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\beta_{n}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|u_{n,m}xy|\geq u\}}F(dx)F(dy).

By
(
4.15
)
, the first term contributes at the limit and the second term vanishes when
n\rightarrow\infty
. Then we get
nK_{n,m}^{2}(h_{u,0})\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}K^{2}(h_{u,0})=\frac{\theta^{2}}{m(m-1)u^{\alpha}}
.

b)

For
u=0
and
v>0
, we have
nK_{n,m}^{2}(h_{0,v})=nK_{n,m}^{2}(h_{v,0})
. Then, by a) we get that
nK_{n,m}^{2}(h_{0,v})\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}K^{2}(h_{0,v})=\frac{\theta^{2}}{m(m-1)v^{\alpha}}
.

c)

For
u>0
and
v>0
,

\displaystyle nK_{n,m}^{2}(h_{u,v})=\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})^{3}}{nm^{2}\lambda_{n,m}^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|z|>\beta_{n}}\hskip-14.22636pt\mathbbm{1}_{\{|u_{n,m}xy|\geq u,|u_{n,m}xz|\geq v\}}F(dx)F(dy)F(dz)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})^{3}}{nm^{2}\lambda_{n,m}^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\beta_{n}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|u_{n,m}xy|\geq u\}}F(dx)F(dy).

Again, by
(
4.15
)
, we have
nK_{n,m}^{2}(h_{u,v})\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}K^{2}(h_{u,v})=0
.

Concerning assertion (ii)
:

We choose
h={h^{\prime}},{h^{\prime\prime}}
where
{h^{\prime}}(x,y)=x\mathbbm{1}_{\{x^{2}+y^{2}\leq 1\}}
and
{h^{\prime\prime}}(x,y)=y\mathbbm{1}_{\{x^{2}+y^{2}\leq 1\}}
. Similarly as above, by
(
H3
)
,
nK_{n,m}^{2}(h)=K^{2}(h)=0
.

Concerning assertion (iii)
:

We choose
h={h}_{1},{h}_{2},{h}_{3}
where
{h}_{1}(x,y)=x^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x^{2}+y^{2}\leq 1\}}
,
{h}_{2}(x,y)=xy\mathbbm{1}_{\{x^{2}+y^{2}\leq 1\}}
and
{h}_{3}(x,y)=y^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x^{2}+y^{2}\leq 1\}}
. First of all, as above in (ii), by hypothesis
(
H3
)
, we have
nK_{n,m}^{2}(h_{2})=K^{2}(h_{2})=0
. Now, by symmetry, we have

nK_{n,m}^{1}(h_{1})=nK_{n,m}^{1}(h_{3})=\frac{e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})^{2}}{nm^{2}\lambda_{n,m}^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\beta_{n}}\hskip-17.07182ptu_{n,m}^{2}x^{2}y^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|u_{n,m}xy|\leq 1\}}F(dx)F(dy)\\
\\
+\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})^{3}}{n^{2}m^{3}\lambda_{n,m}^{3}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|z|>\beta_{n}}u_{n,m}^{2}x^{2}y^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|xy|^{2}\leq 1/u_{n,m}^{2}-|xz|^{2}\}}F(dx)F(dy)F(dz).

By similar estimations as in
(
4.16
)
, we can easily deduce

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nm^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\beta_{n}}u_{n,m}^{2}x^{2}y^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{u_{n,m}|xy|\leq 1\}}F(dx)F(dy)\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\frac{\alpha\theta^{2}}{m(m-1)(2-\alpha)}

which gives the limit of the first term and that the second term vanishes as it is bounded by

\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})^{3}}{nm^{2}\lambda_{n,m}^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\beta_{n}}u_{n,m}^{2}x^{2}y^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{u_{n,m}|xy|\leq 1\}}F(dx)F(dy)
converging to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Therefore,
nK_{n,m}^{2}(h_{1})\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}K^{2}(h_{1})
and
nK_{n,m}^{2}(h_{3})\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}K^{2}(h_{3})
. In conclusion, the obtained limit pair has i.i.d. marginals since it has no Gaussian part and its Lévy measure
K^{2}
is supported on the union of the coordinate axes (see e.g. Ex.12.8 in
[
15
]
).

Overall, by the pairwise independence proven above, we can realize the limit of the vector
(\overline{Y^{\prime}}^{n}_{1}(2),{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{1}^{n}(1,1))
as some vector
(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\overline{Y^{\prime}}^{k}_{1},\sum_{k=2}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}V^{j,k}_{1})
Lévy process with no drift, no Gaussian part and where

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(e^{i(u\sum_{k=1}^{m}\overline{Y^{\prime}}^{k}_{1}+v\sum_{k=2}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}V^{j,k}_{1})})=[\mathbb{E}(e^{iu\overline{Y^{\prime}}^{1}_{1}})]^{m}\times[\mathbb{E}(e^{ivV^{1,2}_{1}})]^{\frac{m(m-1)}{2}}

which is equal to

\exp{\left(\int F(dx)(e^{iux}-1-iux\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\leq 1\}})+\frac{\alpha\theta^{2}}{2m(m-1)}\int\frac{1}{|x|^{1+\alpha}}(e^{ivx}-1-ivx\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\leq 1\}})dx\right)}.

This completes the proof.
∎

4.3.
Asymptotic behavior of the couple
(\overline{Y}^{n},u_{n,m}Z^{n,m})
for case
(C3)
.

For the first component
\overline{Y}^{n}
, we use the same decomposition given by the relation
(
4
)
. For the second one, we consider the formula of
u_{n,m}Z^{n,m}
given in
(
4.3
)
. In this case, the second term from this formula does not contribute to the limit. Therefore, we need only to give the analysis for its first term. To do so, we consider the same decomposition given in
(
4.12
)
.
The two last terms in this decomposition do not contribute on the limit. Then we only have to study
\Gamma_{t}^{n}(1)
and
\Gamma_{t}^{n}(2)
. For the first one, we use the same decomposition as cases
(C2)
and
(C4)
, namely we have
\Gamma_{t}^{n}(1)=\Gamma_{t}^{n}(1,1)+\Gamma_{t}^{n}(1,2)
as given in
(
4.2
)
.
Now, similarly, we separate
\Gamma^{n}(2)
into two terms: the first term that will be the essential term of the limit contains only the first jumps and the drifts and the second term that will be sort in the rest terms contains all the other jumps. Then, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t}^{n}(2)=

\displaystyle u_{n,m}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\sum_{k=2}^{m}\left[\int_{I(nm,i,k)}\hskip-14.22636ptff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})(A^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-A^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})dA^{\beta_{n}}_{s}+\int_{I(nm,i,k)}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})(N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})dA^{\beta_{n}}_{s}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\int_{I(nm,i,k)}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})(A^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-A^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})dN^{\beta_{n}}_{s}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{n,m}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\sum_{k=2}^{m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})\left[\frac{d_{n}^{2}(k-1)}{n^{2}m^{2}}+\frac{d_{n}}{nm}\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}(N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{j+1}_{i}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{j}_{i}})+\frac{d_{n}(k-1)}{nm}(N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t}^{n}(2,1)+\Gamma_{t}^{n}(2,2),

where

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t}^{n}(2,1)=

\displaystyle u_{n,m}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\sum_{k=2}^{m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})\left[\frac{d_{n}^{2}(k-1)}{n^{2}m^{2}}+\frac{d_{n}}{nm}\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t^{j}_{i})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t^{j}_{i})\geq 1\}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 227.62204pt+\frac{d_{n}(k-1)}{nm}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1\}}\right],

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t}^{n}(2,2)=

\displaystyle u_{n,m}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\sum_{k=2}^{m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})\left[\frac{d_{n}}{nm}\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h=2}^{K(t_{j}^{i})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{j}_{i})}+\frac{d_{n}(k-1)}{nm}\sum_{h=2}^{K(t^{j}_{i})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{j}_{i})}\right].

In this case,
u_{n,m}Z^{n,m}_{t}=\mathcal{M}^{n,m}_{t}+\mathcal{R}^{n,m}_{t},
where

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{n,m}_{t}=\Gamma_{t}^{n}(1,1)+\Gamma_{t}^{n}(2,1)\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathcal{R}^{n,m}_{t}={\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(1,2)+{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(2,2)+\sum_{i=3}^{5}\Gamma^{n}_{t}(i)

(4.17)

where
\Gamma^{n}_{t}(5)=u_{n,m}\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}k(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n},Y_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}-Y_{\eta_{n}(s-)})(Y_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}-Y_{\eta_{n}(s-)})^{2}dY_{s}
.
The proof of the following lemma is postponed to Appendix
A
.

Lemma 4.10
.

For the case
(C3)
, we have as
n\rightarrow\infty
, the sequence
(\mathcal{R}^{n,m})_{n\geq 0}
converges uniformly to
0
in probability.

Lemma 4.11
.

For the case
(C3)
, the sequences
(\overline{Y}^{n}(1))_{n\geq 0}
,
(\overline{Y}^{n}(2))_{n\geq 0}
and
({\mathcal{M}}^{n,m})_{n\geq 0}
are tight.

Proof.

First, instead of working with
\overline{Y}^{n}_{t}(1)=\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\sum_{k=1}^{m}(M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k},t_{i}^{k+1}}+\sum_{j\geq 2}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{\scriptsize j}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})\geq j\}})
, it is enough to prove that for each
k\in\{1,\dots,m\}
the triangular arrays with generic terms
y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,1)=M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k},t_{i}^{k+1}}
and
y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,2)=\sum_{j\geq 2}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{\scriptsize j}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})\geq j\}}
are tight.
By property
(P1)
,
(
2.1
)
and Lemma
C.5
, for the first one, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,1)|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=0,\quad\mathbb{E}((y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,1))^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=\frac{c_{n}}{nm}.

and we conclude by using
(
2.1
)
,
c_{n}\leq C\beta_{n}^{2-\alpha}
, the criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
. For the second one, we have

\mathbb{E}(|y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,2)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq\frac{1}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}|x|F(dx)\sum_{j\geq 2}\mathbb{P}(K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})\geq j|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\\
=\frac{\delta_{n}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}(\mathbb{E}(K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})-\mathbb{P}(K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})\geq 1|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}}))=\frac{\delta_{n}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}(\lambda_{n,m}+e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}-1)\leq\frac{\delta_{n}\lambda_{n,m}^{2}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}=\frac{\delta_{n}\lambda_{n,m}}{nm}

(4.18)

and we conclude by using
(
2.1
)
,
\delta_{n}\leq C\log{1/\beta_{n}}
,
\lambda_{n,m}\leq\frac{C}{n\beta_{n}}
(see
(
H

1

α

)
),
\beta_{n}=\frac{\log{n}}{n}
, the criteria
(
C.2
)
and from the second part of Lemma
C.2
.
Therefore, it is clear that for case
(C3)
,
(\overline{Y}^{n}(1))_{n\geq 0}
is tight.
Next, considering
\overline{Y}^{n}(2)
, from
(
4.10
)
and
(
4.11
)
, as
d_{n}
and
{d^{\prime}}_{n}
are bounded by
C\log{1/\beta_{n}}
from
(
2.1
)
,
\lambda_{n,m}\leq\frac{C}{n\beta_{n}}
from
(
H

1

α

)
,
\beta_{n}=\frac{\log{n}}{n}
,
y^{n}_{i}(2)
satisfies
(
C.2
)
ensuring the tightness of
(\overline{Y}^{n}(2))_{n\geq 0}
from Lemma
C.2
.
Finally, we consider
(\mathcal{M}^{n,m})_{n\geq 0}
, equivalently, we prove that
(\Gamma^{n}(1,1))_{n\geq 0}
and
(\Gamma^{n}(2,1))_{n\geq 0}
are tight. Because
ff^{\prime}
is bounded, for
k\in\{2,\dots,m\}
and
j<k
fixed, it is enough to prove that the triangular arrays corresponding to generic terms
\zeta_{i,k,j}^{n}(1)
and
\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(2)
are tight where

\displaystyle\zeta_{i,j,k}^{n}(1)=u_{n,m}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t^{j}_{i})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t^{j}_{i})\geq 1\}},

(4.19)

\displaystyle\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(2)=u_{n,m}\left[\frac{d_{n}^{2}(k-1)}{n^{2}m^{2}}+\frac{d_{n}}{nm}\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t^{j}_{i})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t^{j}_{i})\geq 1\}}+\frac{d_{n}(k-1)}{nm}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1\}}\right].

For the first term, by similar arguments, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(|\zeta_{i,k,j}^{n}(1)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=

\displaystyle u_{n,m}\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})^{2}}{(\theta(\beta_{n}))^{2}}\delta_{n}^{2}\leq C\frac{u_{n,m}\delta_{n}^{2}}{n^{2}}.

Then, the tightness is obtained by
(
2.1
)
that
{\delta}_{n}
are bounded by
C\log{1/\beta_{n}}
,
\beta_{n}=\frac{\log{n}}{n}
,
u_{n,m}=\frac{mn}{(m-1)(\log{n})^{2}}
,
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
.
For the second term, by using property
(P1)
and
1-e^{\lambda_{n,m}}\leq\lambda_{n,m}
, we have

\mathbb{E}(|\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(2)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq\frac{Cu_{n,m}d_{n}}{n^{2}}(d_{n}+2\delta_{n}),

and we conclude by
d_{n}
and
{\delta}_{n}
are bounded by
C\log{1/\beta_{n}}
(see
(
2.1
)
),
\beta_{n}=\frac{\log{n}}{n}
,
u_{n,m}=\frac{mn}{(m-1)(\log{n})^{2}}
, criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
. Therefore, we get
(\mathcal{M}^{n,m})_{n\geq 0}
is tight.
∎

Theorem 4.12
.

For the case
(C3)
, we have

(\overline{Y}^{n},{\mathcal{M}}^{n,m})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}(Y,Z),

where
Z
is defined as
(
3.4
)
.

Proof.

Since
\overline{Y}^{n}
converges pointwise to
Y
when
n\rightarrow\infty
for the Skorokhod topology, then we only need to prove
{\mathcal{M}}^{n,m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Z
. Since
ff^{\prime}
is Lipschitz-continuous, by virtue of Lemma
C.10
or Lemma
C.9
, it is enough to prove
{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{n}_{1}(1,1)+{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{n}_{1}(2,1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}-\frac{{\theta^{\prime}}^{2}}{4}
where
{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{n}_{1}(1,1)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\zeta_{i,j,k}^{n}(1)
and
{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{n}_{1}(2,1)=\sum_{k=2}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(2)
with
\zeta_{i,j,k}^{n}(1)
and
\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(2)
given by
(
4.19
)
. First, concerning
{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{n}_{1}(2,1)
, for
k\in\{2,\dots,m\}
fixed, on the one hand, by using property
(P1)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(2)|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=

\displaystyle u_{n,m}\left[\frac{d_{n}^{2}(k-1)}{n^{2}m^{2}}+\frac{2(k-1)d_{n}}{nm}\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}xF(dx)\right]

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{(k-1)u_{n,m}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(d_{n}^{2}+2d_{n}{d^{\prime}}_{n}).

From
(
2.1
)
and
(
2.4
)
, we
have

{d^{\prime}}_{n}\sim\theta^{\prime}\log{\frac{1}{\beta_{n}}}
and
d_{n}=b^{\prime}-{d^{\prime}}_{n}
.
Therefore, using
u_{n,m}=\frac{nm}{(m-1)(\log{n})^{2}}
,
\beta_{n}=\frac{\log{n}}{n}
and
\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(2)|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})
is non random and independent of
i
, we get

n\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{1,k}^{n}(2))\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}-\frac{(k-1){\theta^{\prime}}^{2}}{m(m-1)}.

On the other hand, using property
(P1)
, the inequality
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2(a^{2}+b^{2})
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{2}F(dx)<\infty
(see Remark
2.1
), we have

\mathbb{E}(|\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(2)|^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq\frac{Cu_{n,m}^{2}}{n^{3}}(\frac{d_{n}^{4}}{n}+d_{n}^{2}).

Since
d_{n}\leq C\log{1/\beta_{n}}
(see
(
2.1
)
), we proceed similarly as above to get
n\mathbb{E}(|\zeta_{1,k}^{n}(2)|^{2})\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0
.
Then, since

\mathbb{V}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(2)\right)\leq n\mathbb{E}((\zeta_{1,k}^{n}(2))^{2})
,
we get
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(2)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\rightarrow}}-\frac{(k-1){\theta^{\prime}}^{2}}{m(m-1)}
and we deduce that

{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{1}^{n}(2,1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\rightarrow}}-\frac{{\theta^{\prime}}^{2}}{2}.

Secondly, concerning
{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{n}_{1}(1,1)
, we prove its uniform convergence in probability by considering for
k\in\{2,\dots,m\}
and
j<k
fixed, the generic term
\zeta_{i,j,k}^{n}(1)
. To do so, we apply Lemma
C.7
to this sequence in which
(\zeta_{i,j,k}^{n}(1))_{1\leq i\leq n}
are i.i.d. Note that the law of
\zeta_{i,j,k}^{n}(1)
does not depend on parameters
j
and
k
, then we denote it by
K_{n,m}
. We will prove that
nK_{n,m}(h)
converges as
n\rightarrow\infty
for some suitable function
h
corresponding to each assertions of Lemma
C.7
.
Therefore, we have

nK_{n,m}(h)=\frac{(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})^{2}}{nm^{2}\lambda_{n,m}^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{\{|y|>\beta_{n}\}}h(u_{n,m}xy)F(dy)F(dx).

Concerning assertion (i)
:

Here, we choose
h=h_{\omega}
where
h_{\omega}(x)=\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|>\omega\}}
with some
\omega>0
. Using
1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}\leq\lambda_{n,m}
, it is easy to check that

nK_{n,m}(h_{\omega})\leq\frac{1}{nm^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\theta\left(\frac{\omega}{u_{n,m}|x|}\right)F(dx).

By hypothesis
(
H

1

α

)
for
\alpha=1
,
\theta(\beta)\leq\frac{C}{\beta}
and
\delta_{n}\equiv\rho_{n}
, we have

nK_{n,m}(h_{\omega})\leq\frac{Cu_{n,m}\rho_{n}}{n\omega}.

Then, by our choices of
u_{n,m}
,
\beta_{n}
and
\rho(\beta)\leq C\log{1/\beta}
(see
(
2.2
)
), we get
nK_{n,m}(h_{\omega})\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0
.

Concerning assertion (ii)
:

We choose
h=h^{\prime}
where
h^{\prime}(x)=x\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\leq 1\}}
. Using
1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}\sim\lambda_{n,m}
,
u_{n,m}\beta_{n}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0
and assumption
(A)
, namely
F
vanishes outside
[-p,p]
, we have for
n
large enough
\beta_{n}\leq\frac{1}{u_{n,m}p}\leq\frac{1}{u_{n,m}\mid x\mid}
and

\displaystyle nK_{n,m}(h^{\prime})\sim

\displaystyle\frac{u_{n,m}}{nm^{2}}\int_{\mid x\mid>\beta_{n}}\hskip-8.5359ptx\int_{\beta_{n}<\mid y\mid\leq\frac{1}{u_{n,m}\mid x\mid}}\hskip-28.45274ptyF(dy)F(dx)=\frac{u_{n,m}}{nm^{2}}\left({d^{\prime}}_{n}^{2}-\int_{\mid x\mid>\beta_{n}}\hskip-8.5359ptx\int_{\mid y\mid>\frac{1}{u_{n,m}\mid x\mid}}\hskip-14.22636ptyF(dy)F(dx)\right).

Since
u_{n,m}=\frac{nm}{(m-1)(\log{n})^{2}}
and using
{d^{\prime}}_{n}\sim\theta^{\prime}\log{1/\beta_{n}}
(see
(
2.4
)
), the first term in the r.h.s. is equivalent to
\frac{\theta^{\prime 2}}{m(m-1)}
. Now, taking
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1)
such that for
\beta\in(0,\varepsilon^{\prime})
we have
|\frac{{d^{\prime}}(\beta)}{\log{(1/\beta)}\theta^{\prime}}-1|\leq\varepsilon
. Considering the second term in the r.h.s., as for
n
large enough
\frac{1}{u_{n,m}\varepsilon^{\prime}}\geq\beta_{n}
, we rewrite it as the sum of
x_{n}
and
y_{n}
with

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}x_{n}=\frac{u_{n,m}}{nm^{2}}\int_{1/(u_{n,m}\varepsilon^{\prime})\geq\mid x\mid>\beta_{n}}x\int_{\mid y\mid>\frac{1}{u_{n,m}\mid x\mid}}yF(dy)F(dx)\\
y_{n}=\frac{u_{n,m}}{nm^{2}}\int_{\mid x\mid>1/(u_{n,m}\varepsilon^{\prime})}x\int_{\mid y\mid>\frac{1}{u_{n,m}\mid x\mid}}yF(dy)F(dx).\end{array}\right.

First, using
\frac{1}{u_{n,m}|x|}\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}
,
\delta(.)
is decreasing and
\delta_{n}\leq C\log{n}
from
(
2.1
)
we derive that

\displaystyle|x_{n}|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{u_{n,m}}{nm^{2}}\int_{1/(u_{n,m}\varepsilon^{\prime})\geq\mid x\mid>\beta_{n}}|x|F(dx)\delta(\varepsilon^{\prime})\leq\frac{Cu_{n,m}\delta_{n}}{n}\leq\frac{C}{\log{n}},

which converges to
0
.
Second, on the one hand, if we denote

{y^{\prime}}_{n}=\frac{u_{n,m}{\theta^{\prime}}}{nm^{2}}\int_{\mid x\mid>1/(u_{n,m}\varepsilon^{\prime})}x\log{(u_{n,m}\mid x\mid)}F(dx),

then we have
(1-\varepsilon){y^{\prime}}_{n}\leq y_{n}\leq(1+\varepsilon){y^{\prime}}_{n}
which gives
{y}_{n}\sim{y^{\prime}}_{n}
since
\varepsilon
is arbitrarily small. On the other hand, using
{d^{\prime}}(\beta)\sim\theta^{\prime}\log{1/\beta}
(see
(
2.4
)
) we have that
\frac{u_{n,m}{\theta^{\prime}}}{nm^{2}}\log{(u_{n,m})}{d^{\prime}}(\frac{1}{u_{n,m}\varepsilon^{\prime}})
converges to
\frac{{\theta^{\prime}}^{2}}{m(m-1)}
and thanks to
(
2.13
)
we have that
\frac{u_{n,m}{\theta^{\prime}}}{nm^{2}}\int_{\mid x\mid>1/(u_{n,m}\varepsilon^{\prime})}x\log{(\mid x\mid)}F(dx)
converges to
-\frac{{\theta^{\prime}}^{2}}{2m(m-1)}
.
Then, it is clear that
nK_{n,m}(h^{\prime})\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\frac{{\theta^{\prime}}^{2}}{2m(m-1)}.

Concerning assertion (iii)
:

Choosing
h=h_{1}
where
h_{1}(x)=x^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\leq 1\}}
, using the inequality
1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}\leq\lambda_{n,m}
and
c(\beta)\leq C\beta
(see
(
2.2
)
), we have

\displaystyle nK_{n,m}(h_{1})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{u_{n,m}^{2}}{nm^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\beta_{n}}x^{2}y^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{u_{n,m}|xy|\leq 1\}}F(dx)F(dy)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{u_{n,m}^{2}}{nm^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}x^{2}c\left(\frac{1}{u_{n,m}|x|}\right)F(dx)\leq\frac{Cu_{n,m}\rho_{n}}{n}.

Therefore, thanks to our choices of
u_{n,m}
,
\beta_{n}
and using
\rho(\beta)\leq C\log{1/\beta}
(see
(
2.2
)
), we get that
nK_{n,m}(h_{1})
converges to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.
In conclusion, the limit processes have no Gaussian part, a Lévy measure equal to
0
and a drift part equal to
\frac{{\theta^{\prime}}^{2}}{2m(m-1)}
. Finally, we get

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\zeta_{i,j,k}^{n}(1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\rightarrow}}\frac{{\theta^{\prime}}^{2}}{2m(m-1)}
and

{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{1}^{n}(1,1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\rightarrow}}\sum_{k=2}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\frac{{\theta^{\prime}}^{2}}{2m(m-1)}=\frac{{\theta^{\prime}}^{2}}{4}.
This completes the proof.
∎

4.4.
Asymptotic behavior of the couple
(\overline{Y}^{n},u_{n,m}Z^{n,m})
for case
(C5)
.

For the first component
\overline{Y}^{n}
, we use the same decomposition given by the relation
(
4
)
. For the second one, we consider the formula of
u_{n,m}Z^{n,m}
given in
(
4.3
)
. In this case, the second term from this formula does not contribute to the limit. Therefore, we need only to give the analysis for its first term. We have

\displaystyle u_{n,m}\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})(Y_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}-Y_{\eta_{n}(s-)})dY_{s}=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\Gamma^{n}_{t}(i),

where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Gamma^{n}_{t}(1)=u_{n,m}(\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})(M_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}})dN_{s}^{\beta_{n}}\\
\hskip 142.26378pt+\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})(N_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}}-N_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}})dM^{\beta_{n}}_{s}),\\
\Gamma^{n}_{t}(2)=u_{n,m}(\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})(Y_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}}-Y_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}})dM_{s}^{\beta_{n}}\\
\hskip 142.26378pt+\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})(Y_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}}-Y_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}})dA_{s}^{\beta_{n}}),\\
\Gamma^{n}_{t}(3)=u_{n,m}(\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})(A_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}}-A_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}})dN_{s}^{\beta_{n}}\\
\hskip 142.26378pt+\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})(N_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}}-N_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}})dA^{\beta_{n}}_{s}),\\
\Gamma^{n}_{t}(4)=u_{n,m}\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})(N_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}}-N_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{\beta_{n}})dN^{\beta_{n}}_{s}.\end{array}\right.

In this case, the three last terms do not contribute to the limit and we only have to study the first term
\Gamma^{n}(1)
. Let us first rewrite

\Gamma^{n}_{t}(1)=\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\zeta^{n}_{i}(1),

with row-wise i.i.d. random variables
\zeta_{i}^{n},i=1,2,\ldots
defined by

\displaystyle\zeta^{n}_{i}(1)=u_{n,m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})\sum_{k=2}^{m}\left[(M_{t_{i}^{k}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}})(N_{t_{i}^{k+1}}^{\beta_{n}}-N_{t^{k}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}})+(N_{t_{i}^{k}}^{\beta_{n}}-N_{t^{1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}})(M_{t_{i}^{k+1}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{k}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}})\right].

Now, using Fubini for the second term, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=2}^{m}[\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}(N_{t_{i}^{j+1}}^{\beta_{n}}-N_{t^{j}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}})](M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}})=\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}(N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}})(M_{t^{m+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{k+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}).

Then we can rewrite our triangular array as follows

\displaystyle\zeta_{i}^{n}(1)=

\displaystyle u_{n,m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})\left[\sum_{k=2}^{m}(M_{t_{i}^{k}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}})(N_{t^{k+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-N_{t_{i}^{k}}^{\beta_{n}})+\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}(N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}})(M_{t^{m+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{k+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{n,m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})\sum_{k=1}^{m}(N_{t^{k+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-N_{t_{i}^{k}}^{\beta_{n}})[(M_{t^{m+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}})-(M_{t^{k+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t_{i}^{k}}^{\beta_{n}})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{n,m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1\}}+\sum_{j=2}^{K(t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{j}(t_{i}^{k})}\right)\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k},

where
\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k}=(M_{t^{m+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}})-(M_{t^{k+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{k}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}})=\sum\limits_{j=1,j\neq k}^{m}(M_{t^{j+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{j}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}})
. Now, we separate
\Gamma^{n}(1)
into two terms: the first term which is the essential term of the limit corresponds to the part with the first jumps and the second term which will be sort in the rest terms corresponds to the part of all the other jumps. In particular, we have
\Gamma^{n}(1)={\Gamma}^{n}(1,1)+\Gamma^{n}(1,2)
where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(1,1)=u_{n,m}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\sum_{k=1}^{m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1\}}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k},\\
{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(1,2)=u_{n,m}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\sum_{k=1}^{m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})\sum_{j=2}^{K(t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{j}(t_{i}^{k})}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k}.\end{array}\right.

In this case, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{n,m}_{t}={\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(1,1)\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathcal{R}^{n,m}_{t}={\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(1,2)+\sum_{i=2}^{5}\Gamma^{n}_{t}(i),

(4.22)

with
\Gamma^{n}_{t}(5)=u_{n,m}\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}k(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n},Y_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}-Y_{\eta_{n}(s-)})(Y_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}-Y_{\eta_{n}(s-)})^{2}dY_{s}
.
The proof of the following lemma is postponed to Appendix
A
.

Lemma 4.13
.

For case
(C5)
, we have as
n\rightarrow\infty
, the sequences
(\overline{Y}^{n}(1))_{n\geq 0}
and
(\mathcal{R}^{n,m})_{n\geq 0}
converge uniformly to
0
in probability.

Lemma 4.14
.

For case
(C5)
, the sequences
(\overline{Y}^{n}(2))_{n\geq 0}
and
({\mathcal{M}}^{n,m})_{n\geq 0}
are tight.

Proof.

First, we consider the sequence
(\overline{Y}^{n}(2))_{n\geq 0}
given by
(
4
)
. From
(
4.10
)
and
(
4.11
)
, using hypothesis
(
H

1

α

)
,
\lambda_{n,m}\leq\frac{C}{n\beta_{n}}
,
d_{n}
and
{d^{\prime}}_{n}
are bounded by
C\beta_{n}^{1-\alpha}
from
(
2.2
)
with the choice
\beta_{n}=\frac{\log{n}}{n^{1/(2\alpha)}}
, then
y^{n}_{i}(2)
satisfies
(
C.2
)
ensuring the tightness from the second part of Lemma
C.2
.
Now, we recall that
\mathcal{M}^{n,m}_{t}={\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(1,1)
given by
(
4.4
)
and as
ff^{\prime}
is bounded, for
k,j\in\{1,\dots,m\}
fixed and
j\neq k
, it is enough to prove that the triangular array with the generic term
\zeta_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,1)=u_{n,m}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1\}}(M_{t^{j+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{j}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}})
is tight.
By using Property
(P2)
and the independence of the increments, for
|u|\leq 1
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(e^{\texttt{i}u\zeta^{n}_{i,k,j}(1,1)}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})

\displaystyle=e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}+\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\texttt{i}uu_{n,m}x(M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{j+1}_{i}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{j}_{i}})}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}+\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)e^{z_{n,m}(x,u)}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{nm\lambda_{n,m}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)(e^{z_{n,m}(x,u)}-1),

where
z_{n,m}(x,u)=\frac{1}{nm}\int_{|y|\leq\beta_{n}}(e^{\texttt{i}uu_{n,m}xy}-1-\texttt{i}uu_{n,m}xy)F(dy)
.
By applying
[
15
, Lemma 8.6]
for first and second orders, we get
|e^{\texttt{i}uu_{n,m}xy}-1-\texttt{i}uu_{n,m}xy|\leq C|uu_{n,m}xy|\wedge|uu_{n,m}xy|^{2}
. As
u_{n,m}\beta_{n}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\infty
, for
n
large enough, combining these results with
\delta(\beta)\leq C\beta^{1-\alpha}
and
c(\beta)\leq C\beta^{2-\alpha}
(see
(
2.2
)
), we have

|z_{n,m}(x,u)|\leq\frac{C}{n}\int_{|y|\leq\beta_{n}}(|uu_{n,m}xy|\wedge|uu_{n,m}xy|^{2})F(dy)\\
=\frac{C}{n}|uu_{n,m}x|\int_{\beta_{n}\geq|y|>1/uu_{n,m}|x|)}\hskip-28.45274pt|y|F(dy)+\frac{C}{n}|uu_{n,m}x|^{2}\int_{|y|\leq 1/(uu_{n,m}|x|)}\hskip-28.45274pty^{2}F(dy)\leq\frac{C}{n}|uu_{n,m}x|^{\alpha}.

(4.23)

Then, for
u_{n,m}=\left[\frac{mn}{(m-1)\log{n}}\right]^{1/\alpha}
, the suprema of
|z_{n,m}(x,u)|
over all
|x|\leq p
and
|u|\leq 1
goes to
0
as
n
tends to infinity.
Therefore, using
|e^{z_{n,m}(x,u)}-1|\leq C|z_{n,m}(x,u)|
for
n
large enough by
(
C.3
)
,
\rho_{n}\leq C\log{\frac{1}{\beta_{n}}}
(see
(
2.2
)
) and
1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}\leq\lambda_{n,m}
, we deduce that

|\mathbb{E}(e^{\texttt{i}u\zeta^{n}_{i,k,j}(1,1)}|\mathcal{F}_{t_{i}^{1}})-1|\leq\frac{C|u|^{\alpha}u_{n,m}^{\alpha}\log{\frac{1}{\beta_{n}}}}{n^{2}}.

Then,
\zeta^{n}_{i,k,j}(1,1)
satisfies
(
C.10
)
with
\xi{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{n,u}=\frac{C|u|^{\alpha}u_{n,m}^{\alpha}}{n}\log{\frac{1}{\beta_{n}}}
which is bounded by
C
for all
|u|\leq 1
. Thus, combining Lemma
C.3
and the second part of Lemma
C.2
, we get the tightness of
(\mathcal{M}^{n,m})_{n\geq 0}
.
∎

Theorem 4.15
.

For case
(C5)
, we have

\displaystyle(\overline{Y}^{n}(2),{\mathcal{M}}^{n,m})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}}}{{\longrightarrow}}(Y,Z),

(4.24)

where
Z
is defined as
(
3.6
)
.

Proof.

First, we denote
{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{n}_{t}(1,1)=u_{n,m}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1\}}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k}
. Then, as
ff^{\prime}
is Lipschitz-continuous, by virtue of Lemma
C.10
, in order to prove the convergence in law of the pair
(\overline{Y}^{n}(2),\mathcal{M}^{n,m})
, it is enough to consider the convergence of the pair
(\overline{Y}^{n}_{1}(2),{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{n}_{1}(1,1))
with
\overline{Y}^{n}_{1}(2)
given in
(
4
)
.
By the independence structure, for
u
and
v
in
\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(e^{\texttt{i}(u\overline{Y}^{n}_{1}(2)+v{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{n}_{1}(1,1))})=e^{\texttt{i}ud_{n}}\mathbb{E}(e^{\texttt{i}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1\}}(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\texttt{i}ud_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}(e^{\texttt{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1\}}(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k})}).

For
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
fixed, again by tower property,
\mathbb{E}(e^{\texttt{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1\}}(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k})})
equals to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{E}(e^{\texttt{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1\}}(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k})}|\sigma(M_{t^{j+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{j}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}},j\in\{1,\dots,m\})))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\prod_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}(e^{\texttt{i}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1\}}(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k})}|\sigma(M_{t^{j+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{j}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}},j\in\{1,\dots,m\}))).

For
k\in\{1,\dots,m\}
fixed, note that
\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k}
is the martingale part of the small jumps which is
\sigma(M_{t^{j+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{j}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}},j\in\{1,\dots,m\})
-measurable, independent of
K(t_{i}^{k})
and
\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t^{k}_{i})}
then by
(P1)
we get that equals to

\mathbb{E}(e^{\texttt{i}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t^{k}_{i})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1\}}(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k})}|\sigma(M_{t^{j+1}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}}-M_{t^{j}_{i}}^{\beta_{n}},j\in\{1,\dots,m\})\vee\mathcal{F}_{t_{i}^{k}})\\
=e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}+\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k})}F(dx).

Therefore, using the independence structure of
(\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k})_{i\in\{1,\dots,n\}}
we can easily see by
(P2)
that for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}

\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{i,k}
has the same distribution as
\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k}
. Thus, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(e^{\texttt{i}(u\overline{Y}^{n}_{1}(2)+v{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{n}_{1}(1,1))})=e^{\texttt{i}ud_{n}}\left[\mathbb{E}(\prod_{k=1}^{m}(1+\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1))F(dx))\right]^{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\texttt{i}ud_{n}}\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\prod_{k=1}^{m}\exp{\left(\log{(1+\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1)F(dx))}\right)}\right)\right]^{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\texttt{i}ud_{n}}\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\exp{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\log{(1+\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1)F(dx))}\right)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\texttt{i}ud_{n}}\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\exp{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)(e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1)+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathcal{R}_{1,k}^{n,m}\right)}\right)\right]^{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\texttt{i}ud_{n}}\left[\mathbb{E}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)(e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1)+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathcal{R}_{1,k}^{n,m}+\mathcal{R}_{1}^{n}\right)\right]^{n},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1,k}^{n,m}=\log{\left(1+\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)(e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1)\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)(e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}^{n}=\exp{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)(e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1)+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathcal{R}_{1,k}^{n,m}\right)}-1

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)(e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1)-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathcal{R}_{1,k}^{n,m}.

Now, thanks to property
(P2)
we have that
\mathbb{E}(e^{\texttt{i}(u\overline{Y}^{n}_{1}(2)+v{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{n}_{1}(1,1))})
equals to

\displaystyle e^{\texttt{i}ud_{n}}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(\mathbb{E}(e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})})-1)F(dx)+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{R}_{1,k}^{n,m})+\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{R}_{1}^{n})\right)^{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\texttt{i}ud_{n}}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(e^{\texttt{i}ux}\prod_{j=1,j\neq k}^{m}\mathbb{E}(e^{\texttt{i}vu_{n,m}x(M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{j+1}^{1}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{j}^{1}})})-1)F(dx)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 312.9803pt+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{R}_{1,k}^{n,m})+\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{R}_{1}^{n})\right)^{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\texttt{i}ud_{n}}\left(1+\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{n\lambda_{n,m}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(e^{\texttt{i}ux+(m-1)z_{n,m}(x,v)}-1)F(dx)+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{R}_{1,k}^{n,m})+\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{R}_{1}^{n})\right)^{n},

(4.25)

where

z_{n,m}(x,v)=\frac{1}{nm}\int_{|y|\leq\beta_{n}}(e^{\texttt{i}vu_{n,m}xy}-1-\texttt{i}vu_{n,m}xy)F(dy).
Now, using
|e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1|\leq 2
and
1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}\leq\lambda_{n,m}
, then, it is easy to check that for
n
large enough, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1)F(dx)\right|\leq C\lambda_{n,m}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

From this, for any
k\in\{1,\dots,m\}
, using the first evaluation in
C.3
and
(
H

1

α

)
, we have

\displaystyle n|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{R}_{1,k}^{n,m})|

\displaystyle\leq n\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{nm\lambda_{n,m}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}|e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1|F(dx)\right]^{2}\leq C\frac{(\theta(\beta_{n}))^{2}}{n}\leq\frac{C}{n\beta_{n}^{2\alpha}}

which converges to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
by the choice
\beta_{n}=\frac{\log{n}}{n^{1/(2\alpha)}}
.
Similarly, by the second evaluation in
C.3
, we have

\displaystyle n|\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{R}_{1}^{n})|\leq n\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}|e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1|F(dx)+\sum_{k=1}^{m}|\mathcal{R}_{1,k}^{n,m}|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(n\mathbb{E}(\frac{1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}}{nm\lambda_{n,m}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\hskip-14.22636pt|e^{\texttt{i}x(u+vu_{n,m}\tilde{M}^{n,m}_{1,k})}-1|F(dx))^{2}+n\mathbb{E}(|\mathcal{R}_{1,k}^{n,m}|)^{2}\right)\leq\frac{C}{n\beta_{n}^{2\alpha}}+\frac{C}{n^{3}\beta_{n}^{4\alpha}}

which converges to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
by the choice
\beta_{n}=\frac{\log{n}}{n^{1/(2\alpha)}}
.
Now, concerning the main term inside the bracket of
(
4.4
)
, we have
\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(e^{\texttt{i}ux+(m-1)z_{n,m}(x,v)}-1)F(dx)=A_{n,m}(u)+B_{n,m}(v)+C_{n,m}(u,v)
where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}A_{n,m}(u)=\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(e^{\texttt{i}ux}-1)F(dx),\quad B_{n,m}(v)=\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(e^{(m-1)z_{n,m}(v,x)}-1)F(dx),\\
C_{n,m}(u,v)=\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(e^{\texttt{i}ux}-1)(e^{(m-1)z_{n,m}(x,v)}-1)F(dx).\end{array}\right.

Since
1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}\sim\lambda_{n,m}
, it is enough to prove that the three terms
A_{n,m}(u)+iud_{n}
,
C_{n,m}(u,v)
and
B_{n,m}(v)
converge.

Concerning
A_{n,m}(u)+\texttt{i}ud_{n}
.

As
d_{n}=b-\int_{\beta_{n}<|x|\leq 1}xF(dx)
, this term is equal to

\displaystyle\texttt{i}ub+\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(e^{\texttt{i}ux}-1-\texttt{i}ux\mathbbm{1}_{|x|\leq 1})F(dx)\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\texttt{i}ub+\int(e^{\texttt{i}ux}-1-\texttt{i}ux1_{|x|\leq 1})F(dx).

Concerning
C_{n,m}(u,v)
.

By
(
4.23
)
,
|z_{n,m}(x,v)|\leq\frac{C}{n}|vu_{n,m}x|^{\alpha}
and the suprema of
|z_{n,m}(x,v)|
over all
|x|\leq p
and
|v|\leq 1
go to
0
as
n
tends to
0
.
Now, using
C.3
, we have
|e^{(m-1)z_{n,m}(x,v)}-1|\leq C|z_{n,m}(x,v)|
and
|e^{iux}-1|\leq C|ux|
. Then, since
x\mapsto|x|^{\alpha+1}
is
F
-integrable as
\alpha>1
(see Remark
2.1
), we get
|C_{n,m}(u,v)|\leq\frac{C}{nm}|u||v|^{\alpha}u_{n,m}^{\alpha}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

Concerning
B_{n,m}(v)

We rewrite
B_{n,m}(v)={B^{\prime}}_{n,m}(v)+{B^{\prime\prime}}_{n,m}(v)
, with

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{B^{\prime}}_{n,m}(v)=\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(m-1)z_{n,m}(x,v)F(dx),\\
{B^{\prime\prime}}_{n,m}(v)=\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}(e^{(m-1)z_{n,m}(x,v)}-1-(m-1)z_{n,m}(x,v))F(dx).\end{array}\right.

First, by same arguments as above, we get
|{B^{\prime\prime}}_{n,m}(v)|\leq\frac{C}{n^{2}}|v|^{2\alpha}u_{n,m}^{2\alpha}
, hence,

{B^{\prime\prime}}_{n,m}(v)\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

Second, note that
{B^{\prime}}_{n,m}(v)=\int(e^{ivx}-1-ivx)K_{n,m}(dx)
, where

\displaystyle K_{n,m}(h)

\displaystyle=\frac{m-1}{nm}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|\leq\beta_{n}}h(u_{n,m}xy)F(dy)F(dx)

with some function
h
.
We will prove that

\int(e^{\texttt{i}vx}-1-\texttt{i}vx)K_{n,m}(dx)\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\int(e^{\texttt{i}vx}-1-\texttt{i}vx)K(dx),

with

K(dx)=\frac{\alpha}{2}((\theta_{+}^{2}+\theta_{-}^{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x>0\}}+2\theta_{+}\theta_{-}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x<0\}})\frac{1}{|x|^{1+\alpha}}dx.

To do so, we use Theorem
C.6
, then it is reduced to prove that
K_{n,m}(h)\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}K(h)
for
h
equal either to
h_{\omega}=\mathbbm{1}_{(\omega,\infty)}
for
\omega>0
, or
{h^{\prime}}_{\omega}=\mathbbm{1}_{(-\infty,-\omega)}
for
\omega>0
, or
h^{\prime}(x)=x^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\leq 1\}}
, or
h^{\prime\prime}(x)=x\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|>1\}}
.

\bullet

First case
h=h_{\omega}

Since
u_{n,m}\beta_{n}^{2}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\infty
, then for
n
large enough such that
\frac{\omega}{\beta_{n}u_{n,m}}<\beta_{n}
.
Then, we rewrite
K_{n,m}(h_{\omega})=y_{n,m}^{1}+y_{n,m}^{2},
where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}y_{n,m}^{1}=\frac{m-1}{nm}\int_{x>\beta_{n}}(\theta_{+}(\frac{\omega}{u_{n,m}x})-\theta_{+}(\beta_{n}))F(dx)\\
y_{n,m}^{2}=\frac{m-1}{nm}\int_{x<-\beta_{n}}(\theta_{-}(\frac{-\omega}{u_{n,m}x})-\theta_{-}(\beta_{n}))F(dx).\end{array}\right.

Note that by
(
H

1

α

)
we have
\frac{(\theta_{\pm}(\beta_{n}))^{2}}{n}\leq\frac{C}{n\beta_{n}^{2\alpha}}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0
.
Further, as
\frac{\omega}{\beta_{n}u_{n,m}x}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0
uniformly on
\{x>\beta_{n}\}
, using
(
H

2

α

)
,
\frac{\rho_{+}(\beta_{n})}{\log{(1/\beta_{n})}}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\alpha\theta_{+}
(see
(
2.4
)
), we get

y_{n,m}^{1}\sim\frac{m-1}{nm}\int_{x>\beta_{n}}\frac{\theta_{+}u_{n,m}^{\alpha}x^{\alpha}}{\omega^{\alpha}}F(dx)\sim\frac{\alpha\theta_{+}^{2}}{\omega^{\alpha}}\frac{(m-1)u_{n,m}^{\alpha}}{nm}\log{\frac{1}{\beta_{n}}}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\frac{\theta_{+}^{2}}{2\omega^{\alpha}}
by the choices
u_{n,m}=\left[\frac{mn}{(m-1)\log{n}}\right]^{1/\alpha}
and
\beta_{n}=\frac{\log{n}}{n^{1/(2\alpha)}}
.
Similarly, we have
{y}_{n,m}^{2}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\frac{\theta_{-}^{2}}{2\omega^{\alpha}}
, so
K_{n,m}(h_{\omega})\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}K(h_{\omega})
.

\bullet

Second case
h={h^{\prime}}_{\omega}

At first, similarly, we rewrite
K_{n,m}(h_{\omega})={y^{\prime}}_{n,m}^{1}+{y^{\prime}}_{n,m}^{2},
where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{y^{\prime}}_{n,m}^{1}=\frac{m-1}{nm}\int_{x>\beta_{n}}(\theta_{-}(\frac{\omega}{u_{n,m}x})-\theta_{-}(\beta_{n}))F(dx)\\
{y^{\prime}}_{n,m}^{2}=\frac{m-1}{nm}\int_{x<-\beta_{n}}(\theta_{+}(\frac{-\omega}{u_{n,m}x})-\theta_{+}(\beta_{n}))F(dx).\end{array}\right.

Using the same arguments as above, we easily get
{y^{\prime}}_{n,m}^{1}\sim\frac{m-1}{nm}\int_{x>\beta_{n}}\frac{\theta_{-}u_{n,m}^{\alpha}x^{\alpha}}{(-\omega)^{\alpha}}F(dx)\sim\frac{\alpha\theta_{-}\theta_{+}u_{n,m}^{\alpha}(m-1)}{(-\omega)^{\alpha}nm}\log{1/\beta_{n}}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\frac{\theta_{+}\theta_{-}}{2(-\omega)^{\alpha}}
and also similarly
{y^{\prime}}_{n,m}^{2}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\frac{\theta_{+}\theta_{-}}{2(-\omega)^{\alpha}}
. Therefore, we have that
K_{n,m}({h^{\prime}}_{\omega})\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}K({h^{\prime}}_{\omega})
.

\bullet

Third case
h=h^{\prime}

For
n
large enough such that
\frac{1}{\beta_{n}u_{n,m}}<\beta_{n}
, as
\frac{1}{u_{n,m}|x|}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0
uniformly on
\{|x|>\beta_{n}\}
, by
c(\beta)\sim\frac{\alpha\theta}{2-\alpha}\beta^{2-\alpha}
and
\rho(\beta)\sim\alpha\theta\log{(1/\beta)}
for
\beta\rightarrow 0
(see
(
2.4
)
), we have

K_{n,m}(h^{\prime})=\frac{m-1}{nm}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|\leq\frac{1}{u_{n,m}|x|}}u_{n,m}^{2}x^{2}y^{2}F(dy)F(dx)\\
\sim\frac{m-1}{nm}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\frac{\alpha\theta}{2-\alpha}u_{n,m}^{\alpha}|x|^{\alpha}F(dx)\sim\frac{\alpha^{2}\theta^{2}}{2-\alpha}\frac{(m-1)u_{n,m}^{\alpha}}{nm}\log{\frac{1}{\beta_{n}}}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\frac{\alpha\theta^{2}}{2(2-\alpha)}=K(h^{\prime}).

\bullet

Last case
h=h^{\prime\prime}

For
n
large enough such that
\frac{1}{\beta_{n}u_{n,m}}<\beta_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle K_{n,m}(h^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{m-1}{nm}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{\frac{1}{u_{n,m}|x|}<|y|\leq\beta_{n}}u_{n,m}xyF(dy)F(dx)

\displaystyle=\frac{(m-1)u_{n,m}}{nm}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}x\left(\int_{|y|>\frac{1}{u_{n,m}x}}yF(dy)-{d^{\prime}}_{n}\right)F(dx).

At first, note that by
{d^{\prime}}_{n}\leq C\beta_{n}^{1-\alpha}
(see
(
2.2
)
), we have
\frac{(m-1)u_{n,m}{d^{\prime}}_{n}^{2}}{mn}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0
and
K_{n,m}(h^{\prime\prime})\sim\frac{(m-1)u_{n,m}}{nm}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\int_{|y|>\frac{1}{u_{n,m}x}}xyF(dy)F(dx)
for
n\rightarrow\infty
. Then, as
\frac{1}{u_{n,m}|x|}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0
uniformly on
\{|x|>\beta_{n}\}
, using
{d^{\prime}}(\beta)\sim\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1}\theta^{\prime}\beta^{1-\alpha}
,
\rho_{+}(\beta)\sim\alpha\theta_{+}\log{(1/\beta)}
and
\rho_{-}(\beta)\sim\alpha\theta_{-}\log{(1/\beta)}
for
\beta\rightarrow 0
(see
(
2.4
)
), we get for
n\to\infty

\displaystyle K_{n,m}(h^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\sim\frac{(m-1)\alpha\theta^{\prime}u_{n,m}^{\alpha}}{nm(\alpha-1)}\big(\int_{x>\beta_{n}}|x|^{\alpha}F(dx)-\int_{x<-\beta_{n}}|x|^{\alpha}F(dx)\big)

\displaystyle\sim\frac{m-1}{nm}\frac{\alpha^{2}{\theta^{\prime}}^{2}}{\alpha-1}u_{n,m}^{\alpha}\log{\frac{1}{\beta_{n}}}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\frac{\alpha{\theta^{\prime}}^{2}}{2(\alpha-1)}=K(h^{\prime\prime}).

Therefore, we get

{B^{\prime}}_{n,m}(v)\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\int\frac{\alpha}{2}[(\theta_{+}^{2}+\theta_{-}^{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x>0\}}+2\theta_{+}\theta_{-}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x<0\}}]\frac{1}{|x|^{1+\alpha}}(e^{\texttt{i}vx}-1-\texttt{i}vx)dx.

Finally, we have

\mathbb{E}(\exp{(\texttt{i}(u\overline{Y}^{n}_{1}(2)+v{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{n}_{1}(1,1)))})\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\exp\left\{\texttt{i}ub+\int F(dx)(e^{\texttt{i}ux}-1-\texttt{i}ux\mathbbm{1}_{\{|x|\leq 1\}})+\right.\\
\left.\frac{\alpha}{2}[(\theta_{+}^{2}+\theta_{-}^{2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{x>0\}}+2\theta_{+}\theta_{-}\mathbbm{1}_{\{x<0\}}]\frac{1}{|x|^{1+\alpha}}(e^{\texttt{i}vx}-1-\texttt{i}vx)dx\right\},

which completes the proof.
∎

4.5.
Conclusion

The challenge ahead is to apply these approximations to obtain a central limit theorem type for the multilevel Monte Carlo method with the stochastic differential equation
(
1.1
)
driven by a pure jump Lévy process, in the spirit of the ones established by Ben Alaya and Kebaier
[
2
]
and Ben Alaya, Kebaier and Ngo
[
3
]
for the case of a diffusion process, Dereich and Li
[
4
]
for the case of jump-diffusion process and Giorgi et al.
[
6
]
for the case of nested Multilevel Monte Carlo. We keep this work for a future research.

Appendix A
Proof of lemmas
4.4
,
4.7
,
4.10
,
4.13
concerning the rest terms

Note that throughout this section,
C
is a generic constant (may depending on
m
) which can be changed from line to line.

A.1.
Proof of Lemma
4.4

Here, we prove that
the sequences of processes
(\overline{Y}^{n}(1))_{n\geq 0}
and
(\mathcal{R}^{n,m})_{n\geq 0}
converge uniformly in probability to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. First, instead of considering the form
\overline{Y}^{n}_{t}(1)=\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\sum_{k=1}^{m}(M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k},t_{i}^{k+1}}+\sum_{j\geq 2}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{\scriptsize j}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})\geq j\}})
, it is enough to prove that for each
k\in\{1,\dots,m\}
the triangular arrays with generic terms
y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,1)=M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k},t_{i}^{k+1}}
and
y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,2)=\sum_{j\geq 2}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{\scriptsize j}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})\geq j\}}
converge in probability to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.
By Property
(P1)
,
(
2.1
)
and Lemma
C.5
, for the first one, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,1)|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=0,\quad\mathbb{E}((y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,1))^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=\frac{c_{n}}{nm}

(A.1)

and therefore we conclude using
(
2.1
)
,
c_{n}\leq C\beta_{n}^{2-\alpha}
, the criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
. For the second one, we have

\mathbb{E}(|y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,2)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq\frac{1}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}|x|F(dx)\sum_{j\geq 2}\mathbb{P}(K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})\geq j|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\\
=\frac{\delta_{n}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}(\mathbb{E}(K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})-\mathbb{P}(K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})\geq 1|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}}))=\frac{\delta_{n}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}(\lambda_{n,m}+e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}-1)\leq\frac{\delta_{n}\lambda_{n,m}^{2}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}=\frac{\delta_{n}\lambda_{n,m}}{nm}

(A.2)

and therefore we conclude by using
(
2.1
)
, the boundedness of
\delta_{n}
in case
(C1)
, the criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
.
Thus, it is clear that for case
(C1)
,
\overline{Y}^{n}(1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\rightarrow}}0
.
Now, by using the formula of the rest term given by
(
4.1
)
, we have

\mathcal{R}^{n,m}_{t}={\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(2,2)+{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(2,3)+\sum_{i=3}^{5}\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})d\Gamma_{s}^{n}(i)\\
+\overline{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(1,2)+\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}k(X^{n}_{\eta_{n}(s-)},Y_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}-Y_{\eta_{n}(s-)})d\overline{\Gamma}^{n}_{s}(3)+\overline{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(4)+\overline{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(5).

According to Theorem
C.1
(iii), in order to prove the convergence of the third and the fifth terms in the r.h.s. of the above relation, we only need to prove the convergence of each
\Gamma^{n}(i)
,
i\in\{3,4,5\}
and
\overline{\Gamma}^{n}(3)
to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Now, we prove that each term converges uniformly in probability to
0
when
n\rightarrow\infty
.

The term
{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(2,2)
:
Let us rewrite
{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(2,2)=\sum_{k=2}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(2,2)
with

\displaystyle{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(2,2)

\displaystyle=\frac{u_{n,m}d_{n}}{nm}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})((k-1)\sum_{h=2}^{K(t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t_{i}^{k})}+\sum_{h=2}^{K(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}).

For each
k\in\{2,\ldots,m\}
, by property
(P1)
,
(
2.1
)
and the boundedness of
ff^{\prime}
, similarly to the calculations in
(
A.2
)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(|{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(2,2)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq

\displaystyle C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|\delta_{n}}{n\theta(\beta_{n})}[(k-1)(\lambda_{n,m}+e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}-1)+(k-1)\lambda_{n,m}+e^{-(k-1)\lambda_{n,m}}-1]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|\delta_{n}\lambda_{n,m}}{n^{2}}.

Then we conclude by using
(
2.1
)
, the boundedness of
|d_{n}|
and
\delta_{n}
in case
(C1)
,
u_{n,m}=\frac{nm}{m-1}
, the criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
.

The term
{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(2,3)
:
Let us rewrite
{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(2,3)=\sum_{k=2}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(2,3)
with

{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(2,3)=\frac{u_{n,m}d_{n}}{nm}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})(k-1)\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})\geq 1\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{1},t_{i}^{k}})\geq 1\}}.

For each
k\in\{2,\ldots,m\}
, by boundedness of
ff^{\prime}
, similar as above, we have

\mathbb{E}(|{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(2,3)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|\delta_{n}}{n\theta(\beta_{n})}(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})(1-e^{-(k-1)\lambda_{n,m}})\leq C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|\delta_{n}\lambda_{n,m}}{n^{2}}.

Then, we conclude similarly that
{\Gamma}^{n}(2,3)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\rightarrow}}0
by the criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
.

The term
\overline{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(1,2)
:
Let us rewrite
\overline{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(1,2)=\sum_{k=2}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\overline{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(1,2)
with

\displaystyle\overline{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(1,2)

\displaystyle=\frac{u_{n,m}d_{n}}{nm}k(X^{n}_{t_{i}^{1}},\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{1},t_{i}^{k})})(\sum_{h=2}^{K(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}(\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})})^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h,h^{\prime}=2\\
h\neq h^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{K(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h^{\prime}}(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}).

For any fixed
k\in\{2,\ldots,m\}
, by similar calculations as above, using the boundedness of the function
k
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{2}F(dx)<\infty
(see Remark
2.1
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(|\overline{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(1,2)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq

\displaystyle C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|}{n\theta(\beta_{n})}\mathbb{E}((K(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})-1)\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})\geq 2\}}+\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}K(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})(K(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})-1)|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|}{n^{2}}(\lambda_{n,m}+\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{n}).

Then, we conclude similarly that
\overline{\Gamma}^{n}(1,2)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\rightarrow}}0
by the criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
.

The term
\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})d{\Gamma}^{n}_{s}(3)
:
This is equal to
\sum_{k=2}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(3)
with

\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(3)=u_{n,m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})(M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})(Y_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-Y_{t_{i}^{k}}).

For a fixed
k\in\{2,\ldots,m\}
, by the boundedness of
ff^{\prime}
, Property
(P1)
,
(
2.1
)
, the inequality
(a^{2}+b^{2})\leq 2(a^{2}+b^{2})
,
\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{2}F(dx)<\infty
, lemmas
C.5
and
C.4
, we have
\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(3)|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(|\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(3)|^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq

\displaystyle Cu_{n,m}^{2}\mathbb{E}((M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\mathbb{E}((Y^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k+1}}-Y^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k}_{i}})^{2}+(N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k+1}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k}_{i}})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\frac{u_{n,m}^{2}c_{n}}{n}(\frac{c_{n}}{nm}+\frac{d_{n}^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}+\frac{1}{nm})\leq C\frac{u_{n,m}^{2}c_{n}}{n^{2}}(c_{n}+\frac{d_{n}^{2}}{n}+1).

Then as
\alpha<1
, we conclude using the boundedness of
|d_{n}|
,
c_{n}\leq C\beta_{n}^{2-\alpha}
,
\beta_{n}=\frac{(\log{n})^{2}}{n}
,
u_{n,m}=\frac{nm}{m-1}
, the criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
.
Therefore, we have
\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(.)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})d{\Gamma}^{n}_{s}(3)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\rightarrow}}0
.

The term
\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})d{\Gamma}^{n}_{s}(4)
:
This is bounded by
C\sum_{k=2}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(4)
with

\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(4)=u_{n,m}|N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}}||N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}|.

Let
k\in\{1,\dots,m\}
be fixed. Using the independence of the increments
N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}}
and
N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}
and Lemma
C.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(|\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(4)|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(4)|\leq 1\}}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=

\displaystyle\frac{u_{n,m}}{nm}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}|x|\mathbb{E}(|N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{1}}|\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{|N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{1}}|\leq\frac{1}{u_{n,m|x|}}\right\}})F(dx)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{Cu_{n,m}\delta_{n}}{n^{2}}\int_{\frac{1}{u_{n,m}\beta_{n}}>|y|>\beta_{n}}|y|F(dy).

Then using the boundedness of
\delta_{n}
,
\beta_{n}=\frac{(\log{n})^{2}}{n}
,
u_{n,m}=\frac{nm}{m-1}
and Lebesgue’s theorem, we easily check that the two first points of the criteria
(
C.2
)
are satisfied.
In order to prove the third point of this criteria, noticing that
u_{n,m}\beta_{n}^{2}{\rightarrow}0
when
{n\rightarrow\infty}
, then for
y>1
we have
u_{n,m}\beta_{n}^{2}<y\Leftrightarrow\beta_{n}<\frac{y}{u_{n,m}\beta_{n}}
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(4)>y|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)\int_{|z|>\beta_{n}\vee\frac{y}{u_{n,m}|x|}}\hskip-28.45274ptF(dz)=\frac{C}{n^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)\theta\left(\frac{y}{u_{n,m}|x|}\vee\beta_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n^{2}}\left(\int_{|x|>\frac{y}{u_{n,m}\beta_{n}}}F(dx)\theta\left(\beta_{n}\right)+\int_{\frac{y}{u_{n,m}\beta_{n}}>|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)\theta\left(\frac{y}{u_{n,m}|x|}\right)\right).

Now, using
(
H

1

α

)
and as
\frac{y}{u_{n,m}\beta_{n}}<1
for
n
large enough, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(4)>y|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n^{2}}\left(\theta\left(\frac{y}{u_{n,m}\beta_{n}}\right)\theta\left(\beta_{n}\right)+\frac{u_{n,m}^{\alpha}}{y^{\alpha}}\int_{\frac{y}{u_{n,m}\beta_{n}}>|x|>\beta_{n}}\hskip-28.45274pt|x|^{\alpha}F(dx)\right)\leq\frac{Cu_{n,m}^{\alpha}}{n^{2}y^{\alpha}}\left(1+\rho_{n}\right).

Then as
\alpha<1
, using
(
2.1
)
,
\rho_{n}\leq C\log{\frac{1}{\beta_{n}}}
,
\beta_{n}=\frac{(\log{n})^{2}}{n}
,
u_{n,m}=\frac{nm}{m-1}
, the third point of criteria
(
C.2
)
is satisfied. Therefore by Lemma
C.2
, we have
\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(.)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})d{\Gamma}^{n}_{s}(4)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\rightarrow}}0
.

The term
\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})d{\Gamma}^{n}_{s}(5)
:
This is equal to
\sum_{k=2}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(5)
where

\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(5)=u_{n,m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})\left[(A^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-A^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})+(N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})\right](M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}).

For any fixed
k\in\{1,\dots,m\}
, by the boundedness of
ff^{\prime}
, the independence structure, property
(P1)
,
(
2.1
)
, the inequality
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2(a^{2}+b^{2})
,
\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{2}F(dx)<\infty
(see Remark
2.1
), lemmas
C.5
and
C.4
, we have
\mathbb{E}(\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(5)|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(|\zeta_{i,k}^{n}(5)|^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq

\displaystyle Cu_{n,m}^{2}\mathbb{E}((M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}})^{2})\left[\frac{d_{n}^{2}(k-1)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}+\mathbb{E}((N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{2})\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{Cu_{n,m}^{2}c_{n}}{n}(\frac{d_{n}^{2}}{n^{2}}+\frac{1}{n}).

Then as
\alpha<1
, we conclude using the boundedness of
|d_{n}|
,
c_{n}\leq C\beta_{n}^{2-\alpha}
,
u_{n,m}=\frac{nm}{m-1}
, the criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
.
Therefore, we have
\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(.)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})d{\Gamma}^{n}_{s}(5)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\rightarrow}}0
.

The term
\overline{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(4)
:
We recall that

\overline{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(4)=u_{n,m}\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}(k(X^{n}_{\eta_{n}(s-)},Y_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}-Y_{\eta_{n}(s-)})-k(X^{n}_{\eta_{n}(s-)},N^{\beta_{n}}_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{\eta_{n}(s-)}))\\
(N^{\beta_{n}}_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{\eta_{n}(s-)})^{2}dA^{\beta_{n}}_{s}.

Since
\frac{\partial k}{\partial y}(x,y)
is bounded on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, it is enough to prove that for each
k\in\{1,\dots,m\}
, the triangular array with generic term
\overline{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(4)
converges to
0
when
n\rightarrow\infty
where

\overline{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(4)=\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|}{nm}|Y^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-Y^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}}|(N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{2}.

By property
(P1)
, the independence between
Y^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-Y^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}}
and
N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}}
, Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality,
\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{2}F(dx)<\infty
, Lemma
C.4
and Lemma
C.5
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(|\overline{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(4)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq

\displaystyle C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|}{nm}(\frac{|d_{n}|}{nm}+\frac{\sqrt{c_{n}}}{\sqrt{nm}})\frac{1}{nm}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}x^{2}F(dx)\leq C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|}{n^{2}}(\frac{|d_{n}|}{n}+\frac{\sqrt{c_{n}}}{\sqrt{n}}).

Then we conclude using the boundedness of
|d_{n}|
,
c_{n}
,
u_{n,m}=\frac{nm}{m-1}
, the criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
. Therefore, we have
\overline{\Gamma}^{n}(4)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\rightarrow}}0
.

The term
\overline{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(5)
:
We recall that

\overline{\Gamma}^{n}_{t}(5)=\frac{u_{n,m}d_{n}}{nm}\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\sum_{k=2}^{m}(k(X^{n}_{t^{1}_{i}},N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})-k(X^{n}_{t^{1}_{i}},\Delta Y_{T_{1}^{\beta_{n}}(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}))(N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{2}.

Similarly as for the term
\overline{\Gamma}^{n}(4)
, it is enough to prove that for each
k\in\{1,\dots,m\}
, the triangular array with generic term
\overline{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(5)
converges to
0
when
n\rightarrow\infty
with

\overline{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(5)=\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|}{nm}\left|\sum_{j=2}^{K(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{j}(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}\right|(N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{2}.

By using Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality, property
(P1)
, Lemmas
C.4
and
C.5
,
\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{4}F(dx)<\infty
(see Remark
2.1
) and the calculations developped for the term
\overline{\Gamma}^{n}(1,2)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(|\overline{\zeta}^{n}_{i,k}(5)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|}{n}\left[\mathbb{E}((\sum_{j=2}^{K(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{j}(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})})^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\times\mathbb{E}((N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{4})\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|}{n^{3/2}}\left[\mathbb{E}(\sum_{j=2}^{K(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}(\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{j}(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})})^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,j^{\prime}=2\\
j\neq j^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{K(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{j}(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{j^{\prime}}(t^{1}_{i},t_{i}^{k})}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|}{n^{3/2}}\left[\frac{1}{n}(\lambda_{n,m}+\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{n})\right]^{1/2}.

Then we conclude using the boundedness of
|d_{n}|
,
\delta_{n}
,
u_{n,m}=\frac{nm}{m-1}
,
\lambda_{n,m}\rightarrow 0
when
n\rightarrow\infty
, the criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
. Therefore we get
\overline{\Gamma}^{n}(5)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\rightarrow}}0
.

The term
\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}k(X^{n}_{\eta_{n}(s-)},Y_{\eta_{nm}(s-)}-Y_{\eta_{n}(s-)})d\overline{\Gamma}^{n}_{s}(3)
:
Since
k(x,y)
is bounded on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, using the inequality
(a+b+c)^{2}\leq 4(a^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2})
, it is enough to prove that for
k\in\{1,\dots,m\}
, the following eight triangular arrays with generic terms
\{\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,j),j\in\{1,\dots,8\}
converge to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
with

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,1)=u_{n,m}(A^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-A^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{2}|A^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-A^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}|,&\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,2)=u_{n,m}(A^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-A^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{2}|M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}|\\
\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,3)=u_{n,m}(A^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-A^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{2}|N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}|,&\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,4)=u_{n,m}(M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{2}|A^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-A^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}|\\
\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,5)=u_{n,m}(N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{2}|M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}|,&\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,6)=u_{n,m}(M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{2}|N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}|\\
\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,7)=u_{n,m}(M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{2}|M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}|,&\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,8)=u_{n,m}(N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{1}_{i}})^{2}|N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}|.\end{array}\right.

For the first four triangular arrays, as
A^{\beta_{n}}_{t}=d_{n}t
is deterministic, applying Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality and Lemma
C.5
for the increment
|M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}|
and
Lemma
C.4
for the increment
N^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}
, it is easy to check

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbb{E}(|\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,1)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|^{3}}{n^{3}},&\mathbb{E}(|\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,2)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}}){\leq}C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|^{2}\sqrt{c_{n}}}{n^{2}\sqrt{n}}\\
\mathbb{E}(|\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,3)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}}){\leq}C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|^{2}\delta_{n}}{n^{3}},&\mathbb{E}(|\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,4)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq C\frac{u_{n,m}|d_{n}|c_{n}}{n^{2}}.\end{array}\right.

Then, for our choices of
u_{n,m}
and
\beta_{n}
, by the boundedness of
d_{n}
and
\delta_{n}
,
c_{n}\leq C\beta_{n}^{2-\alpha}
with
\beta_{n}=\frac{(\log{n})^{2}}{n}
, the application of the criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
is straightforward for these first four triangular arrays.
Now, by using the independence between the increments of
M^{\beta_{n}}
and
N^{\beta_{n}}
, applying Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality and Lemma
C.5
for the estimation of the increment
|M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}|
,
Lemma
C.4
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{2}F(dx)<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbb{E}(|\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,5)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq C\frac{u_{n,m}\sqrt{c_{n}}}{n\sqrt{n}},&\mathbb{E}(|\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,6)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq C\frac{u_{n,m}c_{n}\delta_{n}}{n^{2}}.\end{array}

By the same arguments as above, we get
nc_{n}
converges to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
and then we apply the criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
to get the convergence of these two triangular arrays to
0
. For the seventh triangular array, we use the independence between the two increments
M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k+1}_{i}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}
and
M^{\beta_{n}}_{t^{k}_{i}}-M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{1}}
and Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(|\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,7)||\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq C\frac{u_{n,m}c_{n}^{3/2}}{n\sqrt{n}}.

(A.10)

Then we conclude similarly the convergence of this triangular arrays to
0
.
Finally, concerning the last triangular array, we use similar calculations and arguments as for the term
\int_{0}^{\eta_{n}(t)}ff^{\prime}(X_{\eta_{n}(s-)}^{n})d\Gamma^{n}_{s}(4)
and we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(|\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,8)|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,8)|\leq 1\}}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=

\displaystyle\frac{u_{n,m}}{nm}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}|x|\mathbb{E}(|N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{1}}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{|N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k}}-N^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{1}}|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{u_{n,m|x|}}}\right\}})F(dx)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{Cu_{n,m}\delta_{n}}{n^{2}}\int_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{u_{n,m}\beta_{n}}}>|y|>\beta_{n}}|y|^{2}F(dy),

and using
(
H

1

α

)
and
(
2.2
)
, for
y>1
and
n
large enough, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\overline{\zeta}_{i,k}^{n}(3,8)>y|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n^{2}}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)\theta\left(\sqrt{\frac{y}{u_{n,m}|x|}}\vee\beta_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n^{2}}\left(\int_{|x|>\sqrt{\frac{y}{u_{n,m}\beta_{n}}}}F(dx)\theta\left(\beta_{n}\right)+\int_{\sqrt{\frac{y}{u_{n,m}\beta_{n}}}>|x|>\beta_{n}}F(dx)\theta\left(\sqrt{\frac{y}{u_{n,m}|x|}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n^{2}}\left(\theta\left(\sqrt{\frac{y}{u_{n,m}\beta_{n}}}\right)\theta\left(\beta_{n}\right)+\frac{u_{n,m}^{\alpha/2}}{y^{\alpha/2}}\int_{\sqrt{\frac{y}{u_{n,m}\beta_{n}}}>|x|>\beta_{n}}|x|^{\alpha/2}F(dx)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{Cu_{n,m}^{\alpha/2}}{n^{2}y^{\alpha/2}}\left(1+\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}|x|^{\alpha/2}F(dx)\right)\leq\frac{Cu_{n,m}^{\alpha/2}}{n^{2}y^{\alpha/2}}\left(1+\beta_{n}^{-\alpha/2}\right).

Note that, as
\alpha<1
,
\beta_{n}=\frac{(\log{n})^{2}}{n}
and
u_{n,m}=\frac{nm}{m-1}
, we have that
\frac{u_{n,m}^{\alpha/2}\beta_{n}^{-\alpha/2}}{n}
converges to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Therefore, we conclude the convergence of our last triangular array by criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
.
This completes the proof of Lemma
4.4
.

A.2.
Proof of Lemma
4.7

Here, we prove that
the sequences of processes
(\overline{Y}^{n}(1))_{n\geq 0}
and
(\mathcal{R}^{n,m})_{n\geq 0}
converge uniformly in probability to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. First, instead of considering the form
\overline{Y}^{n}_{t}(1)
given in
(
4
)
, it is enough to prove that for each
k\in\{1,\dots,m\}
the triangular arrays with generic terms
y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,1)=M^{\beta_{n}}_{t_{i}^{k},t_{i}^{k+1}}
and
y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,2)=\sum_{j\geq 2}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{\scriptsize j}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})\geq j\}}
converge uniformly in probability to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. On the one hand, from the above estimates
(
A.1
)
, we have

\mathbb{E}(y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,1)|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=0,\quad\mathbb{E}(|y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,1)|^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=\frac{c_{n}}{nm}.
Then,
y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,1)
satisfies
(
C.2
)
and we conclude using
c_{n}\leq C\beta_{n}^{2-\alpha}
from
(
2.1
)
and Lemma
C.2
. On the other hand, using similar calculations as in
(
A.2
)
, property
(P1)
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{2}F(dx)<\infty
,
we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbb{E}(y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,2)|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=\frac{{d^{\prime}}_{n}}{nm\lambda_{n,m}}(\lambda_{n,m}+e^{-\lambda_{n,m}}-1),\\
\mathbb{E}(|y^{n,m}_{i,k}(1,2)|^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=\mathbb{E}(\sum_{j=2}^{K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})}(\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{\scriptsize j}(t_{i}^{k})})^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,j^{\prime}=2\\
j\neq j^{\prime}\end{subarray}}^{K({\scriptsize t_{i}^{k}})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{\scriptsize j}(t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{\scriptsize j^{\prime}}(t_{i}^{k})}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\\
\leq C\mathbb{E}(\frac{1}{\theta(\beta_{n})}K(t_{i}^{k})\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 2\}}+\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{(\theta(\beta_{n}))^{2}}K(t_{i}^{k})(K(t_{i}^{k})-1)\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 2\}}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq\frac{C}{n}(\lambda_{n,m}+\frac{\delta_{n}^{2}}{n}).\end{array}\right.

Then we conclude using
{d^{\prime}}_{n}=0
by the hypothesis
(
H3
)
,
\delta_{n}\leq C\log{1/\beta_{n}}
(see
(
2.1
)
),
\beta_{n}=\frac{\log{n}}{n}
,
\lambda_{n,m}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
. Therefore, we have
\overline{Y}^{n}(1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\rightarrow}}0
.
Now, from the formula of the rest term given by
(
4.14
)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{n,m}_{t}={\Gamma}_{t}^{n}(1,2)+\sum_{i=2}^{5}\Gamma^{n}_{t}(i).

In what follows, we prove that each term converges uniformly in probability to
0
.

The term
{\Gamma}_{t}^{n}(1,2)
: We recall that

{\Gamma}_{t}^{n}(1,2)=\sum_{i=1}^{[nt]}\sum_{k=2}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}({\zeta}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)+{\zeta^{\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)+{\zeta^{\prime\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)),

with

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\zeta}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)=u_{n,m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t_{i}^{k})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t_{i}^{k})\geq 1\}}\sum_{h=2}^{K(t^{j}_{i})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{j}_{i})},\\
{\zeta^{\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)=u_{n,m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})\sum_{h=2}^{K(t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{1}(t^{j}_{i})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t^{j}_{i})\geq 1\}},\\
{\zeta^{\prime\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)=u_{n,m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}}^{n})\sum_{h=2}^{K(t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t_{i}^{k})}\sum_{h=2}^{K(t^{j}_{i})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{j}_{i})}.\end{array}\right.

Instead of working with
{\Gamma}_{t}^{n}(1,2)
, it is enough to prove that for each
k\in\{2,\dots,m\}
and
j\in\{1,\dots,k-1\}
, the three triangular arrays with generic terms
{\zeta}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)
,
{\zeta^{\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)
and
{\zeta^{\prime\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)
converge uniformly in probability to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.
Concerning
{\zeta}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)
, on the one hand, by property
(P1)
and hypothesis
(
H3
)
, we have

\mathbb{E}({\zeta}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|{\zeta}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)|\leq 1\}}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=0.

On the other hand, as
ff^{\prime}
is bounded, using property
(P1)
, the inequalities

1-e^{\lambda_{n,m}}\leq\lambda_{n,m}
and

(\sum_{i=1}^{n}|x_{i}|)^{\alpha}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}|x_{i}|^{\alpha}
for
x_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\alpha\leq 1
and
c(\beta)\leq C\beta^{2-\alpha}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(|{\zeta}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|{\zeta}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)|\leq 1\}}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})

\displaystyle=\frac{u_{n,m}^{2}(1-e^{-\lambda_{n,m}})}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\mathbb{E}((\sum_{h=2}^{K(t^{j}_{i})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{j}_{i})})^{2}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\hskip-14.22636ptx^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|u_{n,m}ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}})x\sum_{h=2}^{K(t^{j}_{i})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{j}_{i})}|\leq 1\}}F(dx)|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Cu_{n,m}^{2}}{n}\mathbb{E}((\sum_{h=2}^{K(t^{j}_{i})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{j}_{i})})^{2}c(\frac{1}{u_{n,m}|ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}})||\sum_{h=2}^{K(t^{j}_{i})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{j}_{i})}|})|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Cu_{n,m}^{\alpha}}{n}\mathbb{E}((\sum_{h=2}^{K(t^{j}_{i})}|\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{j}_{i})}|)^{\alpha}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq\frac{Cu_{n,m}^{\alpha}}{n}\mathbb{E}(\sum_{h=2}^{K(t^{j}_{i})}|\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{j}_{i})}|^{\alpha}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Cu_{n,m}^{\alpha}}{n}\frac{\mathbb{E}(K(t^{j}_{i})\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t^{j}_{i})\geq 2\}}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\rho_{n}}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\leq\frac{Cu_{n,m}^{\alpha}\rho_{n}\lambda_{n,m}}{n^{2}}.

Now, using similar arguments as above, the inequality
\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}a_{j}|>1\}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|a_{j}|>1/\ell\}}
,
\theta(.)
is decreasing and hypothesis
(
H

1

α

)
, we obtain for all
y>1

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|{\zeta}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)|>y|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq\frac{C}{n}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\mathbb{P}(|ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}})u_{n,m}x\sum_{h=2}^{K(t^{j}_{i})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{j}_{i})}|>y|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})F(dx)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\mathbb{E}(K(t^{j}_{i})\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t^{j}_{i})\geq 2\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}})u_{n,m}x\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{2}(t^{j}_{i})}|>y/(K(t^{j}_{i})-1)\}}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})F(dx)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\frac{1}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\mathbb{E}(K(t^{j}_{i})\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t^{j}_{i})\geq 2\}}\int_{|z|>\beta_{n}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|z|>\frac{y}{|ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}})|(K(t^{j}_{i})-1)u_{n,m}|x|}\}}F(dz)|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})F(dx)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\frac{1}{\theta(\beta_{n})}\mathbb{E}(K(t^{j}_{i})\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t^{j}_{i})\geq 2\}}\theta(\frac{y}{|ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}})|(K(t^{j}_{i})-1)u_{n,m}|x|})|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})F(dx)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\int_{|x|>\beta_{n}}\frac{1}{\theta(\beta_{n})y^{\alpha}}\mathbb{E}(K(t^{j}_{i})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{K(t^{j}_{i})\geq 2\}}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}}){u_{n,m}^{\alpha}|x|^{\alpha}}F(dx)\leq\frac{Cu_{n,m}^{\alpha}\lambda_{n,m}\rho_{n}}{n^{2}y^{\alpha}}.

Then, we conclude the convergence of the triangular array with generic term
{\zeta}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)
using
u_{n,m}=(\frac{nm}{(m-1)\log{n}})^{1/\alpha}
,
\beta_{n}=(\frac{m}{m-1})^{1/\alpha}u_{n,m}^{-1}
,
\rho_{n}\leq C\log{1/\beta_{n}}
(see
(
2.1
)
),
\lambda_{n,m}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, criteria
(
C.2
)
and Lemma
C.2
. Now, concerning the triangular array with the generic term
{\zeta^{\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)
, noticing that
j
and
k
play a symmetric role in
{\zeta}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)
and
{\zeta^{\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)
, the same calculations yield the same bounds for the three conditions of criteria
(
C.2
)
and therefore we obtain the convergence of the triangular array with the generic term
{\zeta^{\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)
in the same way. Finally, concerning
{\zeta^{\prime\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)
, by similar arguments, we have
\mathbb{E}({\zeta^{\prime\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|{\zeta^{\prime\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)|\leq 1\}}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})=0
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}({\zeta^{\prime\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|{\zeta^{\prime\prime}}_{i,k,j}^{n}(1,2)|\leq 1\}}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}})\leq Cu_{n,m}^{2}\mathbb{E}((\sum_{h=2}^{K(t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{k}_{i})})^{2}(\sum_{h=2}^{K(t_{i}^{j})}|\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{j}_{i})}|)^{\alpha+(2-\alpha)}

\displaystyle\times\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\sum_{h=2}^{K(t_{i}^{j})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{j}_{i})}|\leq\frac{1}{u_{n,m}|ff^{\prime}(X_{t_{i}^{1}})\sum_{h=2}^{K(t_{i}^{k})}\Delta Y_{T^{\beta_{n}}_{h}(t^{k}_{i})}|}\}}|\mathcal{F}_{t^{1}_{i}}),
